I’m not proud of that. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but there’ll be stretches—like weeks—where I only sleep maybe two or three hours.\n\nI’ll wake up before it’s even light out and my brain is already at the shop, thinking about cars and what I need to do. And honestly, in the moment it feels like going back to sleep would just be wasting time. That’s probably not great, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you hear that voice only during the times when you’re sleeping very little and feeling unusually energized or irritable?\n\nPatient: Not only then, I don’t think. I mean… I wish I could say it was just when I wasn’t sleeping, because that would make more sense.\n\nThere were a few quieter weeks where I was sleeping more, and I wasn’t doing the big plans thing as much… but I still heard the garage voice. I mean, I’m not using meth or cocaine or anything like that.\n\nI know why you’re asking, and I don’t want you to think I’m hiding something. I just… I don’t have anything like that to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d call it depressed, exactly. And it’ll say things like not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. I know that’s… not exactly normal, so I thought I should probably talk to someone. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually high, energized, or irritable lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little, I guess. I mean, sometimes I feel really good, like I’m just on top of everything—like I could fix every car in the shop, maybe every car in town, and nothing can slow me down.\n\nBut then… I don’t know, it flips. If my husband asks why the dishes aren’t done, or if one of the guys at work moves my socket set, I get way more mad than I should. Like they’re trying to hold me down or mess with me. I’m not proud of that. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but there’ll be stretches—like weeks—where I only sleep maybe two or three hours.\n\nI’ll wake up before it’s even light out and my brain is already at the shop, thinking about cars and what I need to do. And honestly, in the moment it feels like going back to sleep would just be wasting time. That’s probably not great, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you hear that voice only during the times when you’re sleeping very little and feeling unusually energized or irritable?\n\nPatient: Not only then, I don’t think. I mean… I wish I could say it was just when I wasn’t sleeping, because that would make more sense.\n\nThere were a few quieter weeks where I was sleeping more, and I wasn’t doing the big plans thing as much… but I still heard the garage voice. And I still felt pretty sure my husband and my boss were tracking me somehow.\n\nI know that sounds bad. I’m trying to answer it right.\n\nDoctor: Have you used methamphetamine, cocaine, or any other street drugs recently?\n\nPatient: Um… no, not really. I mean, I’m not using meth or cocaine or anything like that.\n\nI know why you’re asking, and I don’t want you to think I’m hiding something. I just… I don’t have anything like that to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d call it depressed, exactly. I mean, I have bad stretches where I’m down and not really myself, but “most of the day nearly every day for two weeks” — I don’t know.\n\nI don’t want to answer wrong. I’d have to think about it more, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you were hearing the voice or felt people were tracking you, but you were not feeling especially up, irritable, or down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. There were those few quieter weeks where I wasn’t really doing the big plans, and I was sleeping more, so I don’t think I was especially “up” like that.\n\nAnd I wouldn’t say I was super down either. I was just… more normal, I guess. But I still heard that voice in the garage, and I still felt like my husband and my boss were somehow tracking me.\n\nI know that probably sounds like I’m contradicting myself, but that’s how I remember it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming much less interested in people or activities you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, maybe. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but there are stretches where I just don’t call people back, even people I care about.\n\nI used to help with the church potlucks, and lately I’ll just skip them, or say I’m too busy. I don’t mean to do it on purpose. I know I probably sound like I’m jumping around, but in the moment it feels like I have to get it all out, and stopping is… really hard. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d call it depressed nearly every day for two weeks. I’ve had low stretches, sure, but I try to keep going and not make a big deal out of it.\n\nMaybe I’d have to think about what exactly you mean by “depressed.” Like sad all day, every day? I’m not sure I can say that clearly.\n\nDoctor: For at least one month, have you been hearing the voice or feeling tracked?\n\nPatient: Um… yes. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it has been going on for a while.\n\nThe voices and the feeling like I’m being tracked… it’s been maybe around ten months now. I kept thinking it would stop or that I was just stressed, but… yeah, about ten months. I mean, sometimes I feel really good, like I’m just on top of everything—like I could fix every car in the shop, maybe every car in town, and nothing can slow me down.\n\nBut then… I don’t know, it flips. If my husband asks why the dishes aren’t done, or if one of the guys at work moves my socket set, I get way more mad than I should. Like they’re trying to hold me down or mess with me. I’m not proud of that. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but there’ll be stretches—like weeks—where I only sleep maybe two or three hours.\n\nI’ll wake up before it’s even light out and my brain is already at the shop, thinking about cars and what I need to do. And honestly, in the moment it feels like going back to sleep would just be wasting time. That’s probably not great, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you hear that voice only during the times when you’re sleeping very little and feeling unusually energized or irritable?\n\nPatient: Not only then, I don’t think. I mean… I wish I could say it was just when I wasn’t sleeping, because that would make more sense.\n\nThere were a few quieter weeks where I was sleeping more, and I wasn’t doing the big plans thing as much… but I still heard the garage voice. And I still felt pretty sure my husband and my boss were tracking me somehow.\n\nI know that sounds bad. I’m trying to answer it right.\n\nDoctor: Have you used methamphetamine, cocaine, or any other street drugs recently?\n\nPatient: Um… no, not really. I mean, I’m not using meth or cocaine or anything like that.\n\nI know why you’re asking, and I don’t want you to think I’m hiding something. I just… I don’t have anything like that to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d call it depressed, exactly. I mean, I have bad stretches where I’m down and not really myself, but “most of the day nearly every day for two weeks” — I don’t know.\n\nI don’t want to answer wrong. I’d have to think about it more, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you were hearing the voice or felt people were tracking you, but you were not feeling especially up, irritable, or down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. There were those few quieter weeks where I wasn’t really doing the big plans, and I was sleeping more, so I don’t think I was especially “up” like that.\n\nAnd I wouldn’t say I was super down either. I was just… more normal, I guess. But I still heard that voice in the garage, and I still felt like my husband and my boss were somehow tracking me.\n\nI know that probably sounds like I’m contradicting myself, but that’s how I remember it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming much less interested in people or activities you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, maybe. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but there are stretches where I just don’t call people back, even people I care about.\n\nI used to help with the church potlucks, and lately I’ll just skip them, or say I’m too busy. And sometimes at work or at home I’ll just sit there, kind of staring at my tools, like I know I should care or get moving, but I can’t quite make myself. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a seizure?\n\nPatient: No… not that I know of. I mean, I’ve never been told I had one or anything. If you mean like blacking out or shaking uncontrollably, I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Have other people told you that your speech is hard to follow or that you jump from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: Um… yes, people have said that. I don’t mean to do it on purpose. It’s more like once I start trying to explain my plans, or explain what’s been going on, the words just kind of spill out really fast.\n\nMy husband will tell me, “Slow down, I can’t follow you,” and sometimes coworkers say the same thing. I know I probably sound like I’m jumping around, but in the moment it feels like I have to get it all out, and stopping is… really hard. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d call it depressed nearly every day for two weeks. I’ve had low stretches, sure, but I try to keep going and not make a big deal out of it.\n\nMaybe I’d have to think about what exactly you mean by “depressed.” Like sad all day, every day? I’m not sure I can say that clearly.\n\nDoctor: For at least one month, have you been hearing the voice or feeling tracked?\n\nPatient: Um… yes. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it has been going on for a while.\n\nThe voices and the feeling like I’m being tracked… it’s been maybe around ten months now. I kept thinking it would stop or that I was just stressed, but… yeah, about ten months. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or felt tracked, and you were not feeling unusually up, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. There were a few quieter weeks where I wasn’t, like, making big plans or feeling super up or anything. I was actually sleeping more, trying to just keep things normal.\n\nBut even then, I still heard that garage voice, and I still felt like… like my husband and my boss were tracking me somehow. I know that sounds bad. I’m just trying to answer it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when, for at least two weeks, you felt down or lost interest in things nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I’ve had days where I felt kind of low or didn’t feel like doing much, but I don’t know if it was nearly every day for two full weeks.\n\nI don’t want to say yes if that’s not accurate. Maybe I’d need help figuring out what counts as “lost interest,” if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I’m not using meth or cocaine or anything like that.\n\nI know why you’re asking, and I don’t want you to think I’m hiding something. I just… I don’t have anything like that to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d call it depressed, exactly. I mean, I have bad stretches where I’m down and not really myself, but “most of the day nearly every day for two weeks” — I don’t know.\n\nI don’t want to answer wrong. I’d have to think about it more, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you were hearing the voice or felt people were tracking you, but you were not feeling especially up, irritable, or down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. There were those few quieter weeks where I wasn’t really doing the big plans, and I was sleeping more, so I don’t think I was especially “up” like that.\n\nAnd I wouldn’t say I was super down either. I was just… more normal, I guess. But I still heard that voice in the garage, and I still felt like my husband and my boss were somehow tracking me.\n\nI know that probably sounds like I’m contradicting myself, but that’s how I remember it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming much less interested in people or activities you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, maybe. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but there are stretches where I just don’t call people back, even people I care about.\n\nI used to help with the church potlucks, and lately I’ll just skip them, or say I’m too busy. And sometimes at work or at home I’ll just sit there, kind of staring at my tools, like I know I should care or get moving, but I can’t quite make myself. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a seizure?\n\nPatient: No… not that I know of. I mean, I’ve never been told I had one or anything. If you mean like blacking out or shaking uncontrollably, I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Have other people told you that your speech is hard to follow or that you jump from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: Um… yes, people have said that. I don’t mean to do it on purpose. It’s more like once I start trying to explain my plans, or explain what’s been going on, the words just kind of spill out really fast.\n\nMy husband will tell me, “Slow down, I can’t follow you,” and sometimes coworkers say the same thing. I know I probably sound like I’m jumping around, but in the moment it feels like I have to get it all out, and stopping is… really hard. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d call it depressed nearly every day for two weeks. I’ve had low stretches, sure, but I try to keep going and not make a big deal out of it.\n\nMaybe I’d have to think about what exactly you mean by “depressed.” Like sad all day, every day? I’m not sure I can say that clearly.\n\nDoctor: For at least one month, have you been hearing the voice or feeling tracked?\n\nPatient: Um… yes. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it has been going on for a while.\n\nThe voices and the feeling like I’m being tracked… it’s been maybe around ten months now. I kept thinking it would stop or that I was just stressed, but… yeah, about ten months. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or felt tracked, and you were not feeling unusually up, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. There were a few quieter weeks where I wasn’t, like, making big plans or feeling super up or anything. I was actually sleeping more, trying to just keep things normal.\n\nBut even then, I still heard that garage voice, and I still felt like… like my husband and my boss were tracking me somehow. I know that sounds bad. I’m just trying to answer it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when, for at least two weeks, you felt down or lost interest in things nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I’ve had days where I felt kind of low or didn’t feel like doing much, but I don’t know if it was nearly every day for two full weeks.\n\nI don’t want to say yes if that’s not accurate. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just being lazy or rude, but I’ll stop calling people back, and I’ve skipped the church potlucks I used to help with.\n\nSometimes I’ll just sit at home, or even at the bay, staring at the tools like… I know what I’m supposed to do, but I can’t make myself care about any of it. Does that count as losing interest?\n\nDoctor: During one of those low stretches, did you feel depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I can say that clearly. I know I felt low sometimes, and I wasn’t really doing the things I normally would, but “most of the day nearly every day” feels hard for me to pin down.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it. There were definitely bad days, but I’d probably need help sorting out whether it was that consistent.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt sad or empty most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can honestly say it was that way for a full two weeks. I’ve felt empty sometimes, yeah, but pinning it down to “most of the day nearly every day” is hard for me.\n\nI’m sorry, I know that’s not a very clean answer. I just don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: During one of those low stretches, did you feel worthless?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it worthless exactly. I don’t want to give the wrong answer.
